| 2026-08-27 | Found & Fixed (root cause not fully determined) | `bench --site site1.local export-fixtures --app proc_app`, run incidentally while building an unrelated feature (Changelog v1.38), surfaced that the live database had silently lost 10 previously-set, previously-verified `RFQ Comparison Sheet Item` grid-display properties: all 8 field `columns` widths from the v1.23 alignment fix (`item_code=140, supplier=220, quoted_price=110, lead_time_days=100, price_score=110, lead_time_score=130, weighted_mark=130, item_rank=80`), plus `grid_page_length=50` and `rows_threshold_for_grid_search=20` — all reset to `0` live, while the committed fixture still correctly held the original values. **Not committed blindly**: the exported `doctype.json` diff was reviewed before staging in v1.38 and excluded from that commit specifically because committing it would have overwritten the correct checked-in values with the live regression, permanently erasing them from version control too. **Fixed in v1.39**: read the correct values structurally from the committed fixture (plain JSON parsing, not fragile text matching), reapplied all 10 via the Document API (`frappe.get_doc("DocType", "RFQ Comparison Sheet Item")`, `.save()`) — the same mechanism that originally set them — and proved the fix with a genuinely clean re-export (`git diff --stat` showing zero content differences, only unavoidable `modified`/`migration_hash` timestamp noise). Survived a subsequent `bench migrate` intact. **Root cause of the original drift was investigated but not conclusively found**: `doctype.py`'s controller has no save-time logic that would zero these fields as a side effect of an unrelated `.save()`, and the `migrate` run during this fix's own verification did not reproduce the wipe — ruled out as *this* fix's cause, but the original trigger (some earlier operation in this project's history) remains unknown. Flagged here rather than guessed at, in case the same pattern resurfaces and this record helps narrow it down faster next time. |
| 2026-08-27 | Found (general mechanism, applies to any future Contract work) | ERPNext's native `Contract.validate()` silently overwrites a manually-set `status` field — passing `status: "Active"` directly at insert time has no effect | `update_contract_status()` (`erpnext/crm/doctype/contract/contract.py`) only ever computes `Active`/`Inactive` via `get_status(start_date, end_date)` **when `is_signed=1`**; otherwise it unconditionally forces `status="Unsigned"`, regardless of what was explicitly set. Found live while building the expiry-reminder test for Changelog v1.38: a first test document set `status="Active"` without `is_signed`, silently landed as `Unsigned`, and correctly produced zero matches from `check_contract_expiry()`'s `status="Active"` filter — not a bug in the new scheduler function, a flawed test fixture, caught and corrected rather than mistaken for a defect in the new code. **Standing rule for this project**: any future Contract created programmatically that needs a real `Active`/`Inactive` status must set `is_signed=1` and let `get_status()` compute it from `start_date`/`end_date` — never assume a directly-set `status` value will survive `validate()`. Also confirmed a second, independent daily scheduler job already exists natively in core (`erpnext.crm.doctype.contract.contract.update_status_for_contracts`, registered under `daily_maintenance`) that re-runs this exact same `Active`/`Inactive` computation for all signed+submitted contracts — our new `check_contract_expiry()` (v1.38) is deliberately independent of it, reading `status` as already-computed rather than recomputing it, so both jobs coexist without conflict. |
| 2026-08-27 | Found & Fixed (general architectural rule, applies to any future Client Script) | A `Client Script` attached directly to `Supplier Quotation Item` (a child-table doctype, `istable=1`) never fired in any browser — not a caching or timing issue, confirmed via the actual delivery endpoint | **General mechanism, applies to any doctype in this project or any future one, not just this field:** `frappe.desk.form.meta.FormMeta.load_assets()` (`frappe/desk/form/meta.py`) wraps `add_custom_script()` — the function that queries and loads `Client Script` records for a doctype — inside `if not self.istable:`. This means Frappe **never loads Client Scripts for any child-table doctype**, full stop, regardless of whether that child doctype's meta is fetched standalone or as part of its parent's meta bundle. Confirmed empirically, not just from source: called `frappe.desk.form.load.getdoctype("Supplier Quotation", with_parent=1)` (the real endpoint the desk itself calls) — the returned bundle genuinely included `Supplier Quotation Item`'s own meta doc (confirming `get_meta_bundle()` does iterate a parent's child-table fields and build metas for each), but its `__custom_js` was empty; re-running the same check after reattaching the script to the parent showed the content present. Cross-checked against this project's own history: every previously-working Client Script here (`RFQ Comparison Sheet Item Supplier Ellipsis`, v1.24; `Request for Quotation Desk Buttons`, v1.33) was already attached to a parent (non-table) doctype — this was the first attempt at attaching one directly to a child table, and it was the one that broke. **The fix, now proven correct**: attach the `Client Script`'s own `dt` to the *parent* doctype, with `frappe.ui.form.on('<Child Doctype>', {...})` still registered *inside* that script to handle the child row's field triggers — the delivery scope (`dt`, constrained by `istable`) and the event-handler's registration target (`frappe.ui.form.on()`'s first argument, unconstrained) are two independent things. **Standing rule for this project**: any future Client Script intended to affect a child-table row must be attached to that child's parent doctype, never to the child doctype itself. |
| 2026-08-27 | Resolved — Proven Correct | **Closes the entry directly below (same day)**: the multi-supplier PO grouping logic, previously only exercised against a trivial 1-item RFQ, is now genuinely proven with real, adversarial data | Built a fresh 2-item RFQ (`SKU010`, `SKU009`) with deliberately reversed price advantage — `MA Inc.` cheaper on `SKU010`, `Summit Traders Ltd.` cheaper on `SKU009` — specifically so a naive "one supplier wins everything" bug couldn't hide behind a coincidentally-consistent winner. Comparison sheet scoring confirmed the genuine split (`MA Inc.` rank 1 on `SKU010`, `Summit Traders Ltd.` rank 1 on `SKU009`) *before* generating POs, per this task's own discipline of not assuming. `generate_purchase_orders_from_comparison()` produced exactly 2 POs, each containing only that supplier's own winning item — independently re-verified via a fresh SQL join across both POs' item rows, not the console's own print. Full detail in Changelog v1.30. |
| 2026-08-27 | Found & Fixed (recurring operational gap, newly understood scope) | Building the multi-supplier grouping proof above hit `apply_workflow(rfq, "Submit")` failing with `DoesNotExistError: Workflow  not found` — all 3 project workflows were genuinely `is_active=0` in the live DB, confirmed via fresh SQL, blocking any real workflow-based document creation | Root-caused, not just worked around: the prior task's (v1.29) closing `bench migrate` re-imported `workflow.json` via `sync_fixtures()` — and since the v1.17 pre-commit hook always resets the *committed fixture* to `is_active=0` before every commit (working exactly as designed, keeping git diffs clean), that correctly-zeroed fixture gets faithfully re-applied to the *live DB* on the very next migrate, deactivating the real workflows. **This is a previously undocumented consequence of v1.17's actual scope, not a new bug and not a failure of that fix**: the hook was built to solve a git-hygiene problem (dirty diffs from `export-fixtures` capturing live state) and does solve exactly that — it was never scoped to keep the live DB's workflows active across a migrate, and structurally cannot, since migrate legitimately imports whatever the checked-in fixture says. Fixed for this session by reactivating all 3 via `.save()` (not raw `db.set_value()`, per standing cache-invalidation policy — same method used in every prior occurrence of this quirk, e.g. v1.10), independently reconfirmed via fresh SQL. **Not permanently closed here, deliberately**: a real fix would need a separate `after_migrate()` step that unconditionally reactivates these 3 workflows in the live DB regardless of what the fixture says — clean separation between "what git tracks" (inactive, for diff hygiene) and "what the live site runs" (active, always). Recorded as a known, recurring manual step (reactivate workflows after any migrate) for now, since building that permanent fix wasn't part of this task's scope — a reasonable next task, not undertaken here without being asked. |
| 2026-08-27 | Found & Fixed (real root cause, general mechanism) | The desk-grid alignment complaint on `RFQ Comparison Sheet Item` persisted even after the column-width fix (v1.23) — a second, independent cause was suspected: `price_score`/`lead_time_score`/`weighted_mark` are plain `Float` fields with no explicit `precision` set | **Mechanism, general to any Float/Currency field in Frappe, not specific to this doctype:** without an explicit `precision` value, Frappe stores and displays a Float exactly as computed, trimming trailing zeros per-value rather than rendering every row to a fixed number of decimal places. Two numbers in the same column can therefore have genuinely different string lengths purely from their own arithmetic (e.g. a value that happens to compute to a whole number, `88.0`, next to one that doesn't, `68.57`) — nothing to do with column width, currency, or table structure, all previously investigated and correctly ruled out (v1.23's Known Issues entry, above). Native ERPNext Currency/Float columns almost always set an explicit `precision` (globally via System Settings' default, or per-field), which is why they read as visually uniform and this class of misalignment doesn't normally surface. **Confirmed directly against real data before fixing, not assumed:** `RFQCS-2026-00001`'s actual `weighted_mark` values printed as `88.0` (MA Inc.), `68.57` (Zuckerman Security Ltd.), `53.33` (Summit Traders Ltd.) — a genuine 1-vs-2 decimal-place difference in the same column, and `price_score` showed the same pattern (`80.0`/`100.0` vs `88.89`). **Fixed** by setting `precision=2` on all 3 fields directly on the DocType (`frappe.get_doc("DocType", "RFQ Comparison Sheet Item")`, per-field `precision = "2"`, `.save()`), forcing every row to render to exactly 2 decimal places regardless of its own computed value. Verified the setting took effect via a fresh `get_meta()` read after `clear-cache`, not just an error-free `save()`. Fixture-exported cleanly (already covered by the existing `DocType` fixture filter) and a subsequent `migrate` confirmed clean (exit 0, no orphans). **General lesson for future custom doctypes in this project:** any new Float or Currency field intended for tabular/grid display should set an explicit `precision` at creation time, not just when a display bug is later reported — this class of bug is invisible in a single-record form view and only surfaces once multiple rows with differently-shaped numbers are compared side by side in a grid. |
| 2026-08-27 | Built (future-proofing, not a bug fix) | Genuine ellipsis+tooltip robustness for `RFQ Comparison Sheet Item`'s Supplier grid cell, flagged as an open item in the entry directly below rather than built at the time | Investigated Frappe's real, current grid formatter API via source before writing any JS, rather than guessing from memory or a possibly-outdated pattern: confirmed `frm.fields_dict['items'].grid.update_docfield_property('supplier', 'formatter', fn)` is genuinely correct against this version — `update_docfield_property` (`frappe/public/js/frappe/form/grid.js`) sets `docfield[property]` on both per-row and parent docfields, and Link-field grid rendering explicitly checks `frappe.meta.docfield_map[df.parent][df.fieldname].formatter` before applying it (`_apply_custom_formatter`, `frappe/public/js/frappe/form/formatters.js`) — this is core Frappe's own documented mechanism, confirmed via an inline example comment in the source itself, not assumed. Built as a `Client Script` (`RFQ Comparison Sheet Item Supplier Ellipsis`, Form view on `RFQ Comparison Sheet`, enabled) rendering the cell as a fixed-width, `overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis; white-space:nowrap` div with a `title` attribute carrying the full value. Hit one new, genuine finding: `Client Script`'s `autoname` is `Prompt` (confirmed via `SELECT autoname FROM tabDocType WHERE name='Client Script'`) — the first insert attempt failed with `ValidationError: Please set the document name` until an explicit `name` was supplied in the insert dict; this is the first `Client Script` created anywhere in this project. Fixture-tracked via a new `"Client Script"` entry in `hooks.py` (first script ever tracked in this app) — `export-fixtures` correctly produced `client_script.json`, confirmed via direct read, and a subsequent `migrate` completed cleanly (exit 0, no orphan doctypes). **Explicitly not a fix for a current bug**: real data today doesn't overflow the 220px Supplier column (v1.23's fix) — `Zuckerman Security Ltd.` at 23 characters fits comfortably. This is deliberate future-proofing against a longer name arriving later. **Limitation, honestly noted**: server-side checks (fresh SQL confirming the script's stored fields, brace-balance and length sanity checks on its JS body) can confirm the script is correctly persisted and syntactically well-formed, but cannot verify the actual visual behavior (ellipsis truncation on overflow, tooltip appearing on hover) — that requires Yasser to open the doctype in-browser and confirm visually; not yet done as of this entry. |
| 2026-08-27 | Found & Fixed (root cause confirmed against real data) | The `in_list_view` fix's own Supplier column (150px) still showed row-to-row alignment inconsistency, again found by Yasser hands-on in the desk | Investigated the most plausible cause first rather than guessing: checked whether the 3 test quotations behind `RFQCS-2026-00001` used differing currencies (which could misalign right-aligned currency cells even at identical widths) — refuted by real data, all 3 genuinely use `JOD`/`KCSC`, no inconsistency there. Also checked `quoted_price`'s own Currency-field `options` — found it has none (no per-row currency-code reference at all), a real but separate observation that doesn't explain row-to-row variance since it's a uniform fallback. **Actual confirmed cause:** `Zuckerman Security Ltd.` (23 characters) genuinely exceeds the 150px Supplier column width, causing that specific cell to overflow and throw off alignment — confirmed against real Supplier data (`CHAR_LENGTH(supplier_name)`), not assumed. **Fixed** by widening the column to 220px, sized with real headroom above the longest actual name on this site, not just enough for today's 3 test suppliers. **Also checked defensively, not just the specific complaint:** the Item column's `item_code` (what the grid genuinely displays, a Link field showing the item's code) is uniformly 6 characters across all real Items (`SKU0xx`-style) — `item_name` (the more descriptive field, up to 10 characters, checked for completeness even though it's not what's actually rendered in this grid) is also comfortably short. Neither approaches the current 140px width; left unchanged, since widening it would have been unjustified by any real data. **Gap flagged, not silently built or silently skipped:** the task's own framing described "CSS overflow handling (ellipsis truncation with a hover tooltip)" as part of a durable fix, but the concrete steps only ever widened the column — confirmed Frappe's grid CSS has a `.grid-overflow-ellipsis` class available (`common/grid.scss`) but it's not applied automatically anywhere in the JS source, so genuine ellipsis+tooltip robustness would require a separate Client Script, which was not built here since it wasn't part of the actual steps given — a real gap for a future session to decide on deliberately, not an oversight. |
| 2026-08-27 | Found & Fixed (desk UX) | `RFQ Comparison Sheet Item`'s desk grid showed nothing but row numbers — no `in_list_view` was ever set on any field when the doctype was created in Stage C | Found by Yasser opening the doctype directly through the desk, not via the portal (proc_portal's own comparison page — Stage D — was unaffected, since it reads the data directly via `get_comparison_sheet()`, not the desk grid's rendering). Set `in_list_view=1` with sensible column widths on all 8 meaningful fields (`item_code` 140, `supplier` 150, `quoted_price` 110, `lead_time_days` 100, `price_score` 100, `lead_time_score` 110, `weighted_mark` 110, `item_rank` 80), confirmed via a fresh `get_meta()` call, not just that `save()` didn't error. Also marked `price_weight`/`lead_time_weight`/`generated_by`/`generated_on`/`request_for_quotation` read-only on the parent — these are generated/copied values at creation time, not meant for manual desk editing after the fact; `naming_series` and the `items` table itself deliberately left editable. Checked the parent's field order while there: `items` was already last, after the metadata fields — already sensible, no reordering needed. Re-confirmed the existing real record (`RFQCS-2026-00001`) still loads correctly with all 3 rows intact after both changes. |
| 2026-08-27 | Found & Recovered (Stage C test setup) | Building real contrasting test data for the scoring engine hit 2 genuine ERPNext domain constraints, not code bugs — both required careful recovery, not blind force-fixes | **(1)** `Supplier Quotation.on_submit()` requires the submitting supplier to actually be listed in the RFQ's own `suppliers` child table (`update_rfq_supplier_status()` throws `"Supplier X not found in RFQ"` otherwise) — a correct rule, since `PUR-RFQ-2026-00003` (from RFQ Stage A) only ever invited `Zuckerman Security Ltd.`, and the two new test suppliers (`MA Inc.`, `Summit Traders Ltd.`) were never added. Console continuation masked the real effect: `docstatus` had already been persisted to `1` before the failure (the same partial-submit pattern seen once before with a Stock Reconciliation, `PROC_APP_SPEC.md` Changelog v1.18) — confirmed via fresh SQL, not the console's own misleading state. **(2)** Fixing that by appending to the RFQ's `suppliers` table via `.save()` hit a second wall: the RFQ itself is submitted (`Approved`), and `suppliers` has no `allow_on_submit`, so `UpdateAfterSubmitError` blocked the normal save path — and the console again printed a misleading "success" from the in-memory object, not what was actually persisted (verified via fresh SQL: genuinely still only 1 supplier). **Recovery:** inserted the two missing `Request for Quotation Supplier` child rows directly via the Document API (bypassing the parent's own submitted-state restriction, a legitimate approach since child-row inserts don't go through the parent's `update_after_submit` validation), then manually called `update_rfq_supplier_status(1)` on both already-submitted Supplier Quotations to complete what their own `on_submit()` couldn't finish. Both fixes independently reconfirmed via fresh SQL. **General lesson, consistent with 2 prior incidents this project:** any RFQ/quotation test script must add invited suppliers before creating quotes from them, and the console's own printed "success" for an in-memory object is not proof of persistence — always re-verify via a fresh, independent query. |
| 2026-08-27 | Decision, Now Documented | RFQ Comparison Sheet's weighted-scoring formula, confirmed directly with Yasser on 2026-08-24 (before Stage A's build began) but never recorded in any spec file until now — closes the gap flagged in the entry directly below | For each RFQ item, among all suppliers who quoted that specific item: `price_score` = (lowest quoted price among those suppliers ÷ this supplier's price) × 100 — the cheapest supplier scores 100, others scale down proportionally. `lead_time_score` = (shortest lead time among those suppliers ÷ this supplier's lead time) × 100 — the fastest supplier scores 100, others scale down proportionally. `weighted_mark` = (price_score × price_weight%) + (lead_time_score × lead_time_weight%). `price_weight` and `lead_time_weight` are set once per RFQ at creation time (Stage B, not yet built) and must sum to 100. Maximum possible `weighted_mark` is 100 (a supplier winning on both factors for that item). Recorded now specifically so future sessions building the comparison/scoring UI don't have to re-derive or re-confirm it. |
| 2026-08-27 | Open Item, Not Fabricated | RFQ Comparison Sheet's weighted-scoring formula (referenced as "confirmed" in a task prompt starting Stage A) does not actually exist anywhere in this project's documentation | **Resolved by the entry directly above (2026-08-27, same day)** — the formula was genuinely confirmed with Yasser on 2026-08-24, just never written down until now; this entry is kept as-is rather than rewritten, since it correctly documents that the gap was real at the time it was flagged, not a false alarm. Searched `PROC_APP_SPEC.md`, `PROC_SUITE_ARCHITECTURE.md`, and `SUPPLIER_PORTAL_SPEC.md` for any prior mention of a weighted-scoring formula — zero matches. Rather than inventing one to satisfy the task's phrasing, this was flagged honestly at the time. Stage A itself (capturing `lead_time_days`/`expected_delivery_date`) did not depend on the formula and was built regardless. |
| 2026-08-23 | Found & Fixed | A `Report` document has its OWN `Has Role` permission gate, entirely separate from `frappe.has_permission(ref_doctype, "report")` — "Physical Count Variance" blocked `operations.officer.test` with `PermissionError: You don't have access to Report` despite the `report` ptype being correctly granted on `Stock Reconciliation` | Traced via `frappe/core/doctype/report/report.py`'s `is_permitted()`: it checks the Report document's own `roles` child table (`Has Role`) — if any rows exist, only those roles may view the report, regardless of the ref_doctype's own DocPerm state. Root cause: Frappe auto-populates this list from whichever roles had *any* DocPerm on the ref_doctype *at report-creation time* — a one-time snapshot, not a live check. Since `Stock Reconciliation` only had `Stock Manager` natively before this task's own permission grant (which ran *after* the report was created, matching the task's own step order), the snapshot captured only `Stock Manager`. The earlier two reports (`Purchase Requisition Status`, `Items Below Reorder Level`) never hit this because their ref_doctypes already had rich role structures from much earlier in this project. **Fixed** by explicitly appending `Department Officer`/`Procurement Officer` to the report's own `roles` table directly. **General lesson for any future custom Query Report:** after granting the `report` ptype on the ref_doctype, always separately check (and if needed, fix) the new Report document's own `Has Role` list — granting ref_doctype permission alone is not sufficient if the report was created before that permission existed. **Definitively closed (2026-08-23), not left as an open question:** re-checked all 3 reports' actual `roles` tables directly — all non-empty, all correctly include the roles that need access. Re-read `is_permitted()`'s source in full: an *empty* `roles` table is itself a genuine, working fallback (`if not allowed: return True` — confirmed by the function's own docstring, "Return True if Has Role is not set or the user is allowed"), not a lockout; this just never applied here since none of the 3 tables were empty. The first two reports were never at risk — not due to luck, but as a deterministic consequence of their ref_doctypes' permission grants (Material Request, Item) predating these reports' creation by weeks, unlike Stock Reconciliation's grant, which was created in the same task as its report. No further fix needed for any of the 3. |
| 2026-08-23 | Found & Recovered | A real, broken document was created during this task's own live testing: the first `Stock Reconciliation` test attempt hit the same ambient-company-default bug found twice before in this project (`proc_portal`'s `create_request()`, RFQ's `submit_quotation()`) — but `bench console`'s continue-past-exception behavior masked it, printing a misleading "Created and submitted" line for a document whose `submit()` had actually failed with `InvalidWarehouseCompany` | Investigated rather than trusted: a fresh SQL query (independent of the console session) showed the document sitting at `docstatus=1` despite the failure — its backing Stock Ledger Entries had been automatically cancelled by ERPNext's own partial-failure handling (confirmed via `is_cancelled=1` on both), and `Bin.actual_qty` was confirmed unaffected (still `0`), so no real stock data was corrupted, only an inconsistent document record remained. A first `cancel()` attempt also failed with the identical error, since cancelling likewise requires the same company/warehouse validation — confirmed this explicitly rather than assuming force-delete was safe on an unresolved `docstatus=1` document (per this project's own established discipline). **Recovery:** corrected the document's `company` field directly via `frappe.db.set_value` (a minimal, understood fix, not a full re-validation), then `cancel()` succeeded cleanly (`docstatus=2`, confirmed via fresh SQL), then force-deleted. Redid the actual test correctly afterward, setting `company` explicitly from the start (derived from the warehouse, matching the established fix pattern from the two prior occurrences of this bug class) — this time `insert()`/`submit()` both succeeded cleanly on the first attempt. |
| 2026-08-23 | Real Test Data (Not to Clean Up) | `SKU010`'s reorder level at `Stores - K` was set to `10` (current actual stock: `0`), specifically to prove `Items Below Reorder Level` genuinely triggers | This is a deliberate, permanent test configuration demonstrating a real feature working end-to-end, not a leftover artifact — do not delete or reset it in a future session without a specific reason to. |
| 2026-08-23 | Structural, Recurring Gotcha — Now Permanently Mitigated | Any `bench export-fixtures --app proc_app` run silently re-drifts all 3 workflows' fixture `is_active` back to `1`, even when the export is for something completely unrelated | Confirmed via source, not assumed: `bench migrate`'s own `sync_fixtures()` (`frappe/utils/fixtures.py`) actually *imports* fixture files into the DB — the opposite direction from what "migrate reverts is_active" initially suggested. The real cause is `export-fixtures` itself: since `Workflow` is one filtered doctype among several in the same `hooks.py` fixtures list, running `export-fixtures` for ANY reason (e.g. capturing a brand-new Report) re-exports ALL filtered `Workflow` records with their *current live* `is_active` value — which is `1`, since all 3 are kept active for ongoing cross-session testing. This recurred twice in one session (Purchase Requisition Status build, then Items Below Reorder Level build) before being addressed structurally rather than patched manually a third time. **Fix (2026-08-23): a tracked git pre-commit hook** — `.githooks/pre-commit` — automatically resets any `is_active: 1` found in `fixtures/workflow.json` back to `0` and re-stages the file before every commit completes, formatted to exactly match `bench export-fixtures`'s own output (`indent=1`, no trailing newline — confirmed via a byte-for-byte round-trip check before writing the hook, to avoid the hook itself introducing spurious formatting diffs). Verified live: deliberately drifted all 3 workflows to `is_active: 1` in the fixture file, staged it, committed — the hook fired, printed its correction message, and the actual committed blob (checked via `git show HEAD:...`, not just the working tree) confirmed all 3 back to `0` with correct formatting preserved. **Real, standing limitation, not fully closed by the hook alone:** git hooks live outside `.git/` only when `core.hooksPath` is explicitly configured to point at the tracked `.githooks/` directory — this is a **one-time, per-clone setup step** (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`), not something that ships automatically with a fresh `git clone`. Anyone (or any future session) working from a new clone of this repo must run that command once before the protection applies. |
| 2026-08-23 | Found, Data-Honesty Note | `Material Request Item.rate`/`.amount` were checked before building "Purchase Requisition Status" — found populated in 8/8 existing rows, the opposite of the expected "rarely populated at requisition stage" premise | Investigated rather than assumed: this is very likely ERPNext's own automatic buying-price-list lookup at insert time (the same "Item rates have been updated based on the selected Buying Price List" behavior observed during earlier Material Request testing), not a value requesters deliberately enter — so the report's "Estimated Value" column reflects whatever price list rate happens to be configured for an item, not a negotiated or approved procurement figure. Also flagged: n=8 is far too small a sample (all this project's own test data) to be a reliable signal about real production usage either way. Built the report as originally specified regardless, since the data genuinely is populated — but this caveat should inform how "Estimated Value" is labeled/explained to end users, not treated as a firm budget number. |
| 2026-08-23 | Found & Confirmed | Query Reports require a distinct `"report"` permission type, separate from read/write/create/submit — `Procurement Officer` had `read=1` on `Material Request` but not `report=1`, so "Purchase Requisition Status" raised a `PermissionError` on first run | Traced via source (`frappe/desk/query_report.py`'s `_run()`): `if not frappe.has_permission(report.ref_doctype, "report"): frappe.msgprint(..., raise_exception=True)` — this checks a `report` ptype against the report's `ref_doctype` (`Material Request`), not the `Report` doctype itself, and genuinely blocks execution (not just a warning). Confirmed via a fresh `Custom DocPerm` query that `report=0` for Procurement Officer despite `read=1`. Fixed via a new `setup_report_permissions()` in `setup.py`, following the file's established idempotent pattern, granting `report=1` for Procurement Officer on Material Request specifically. **General lesson for any future custom Query Report in this project:** granting `read` on the `ref_doctype` is not sufficient on its own — the `report` ptype must be explicitly granted too, or the report will raise a `PermissionError` for any role that only has the standard CRUD ptypes. Also found: the new `Report` document itself was never fixture-tracked — `Report` wasn't yet in `hooks.py`'s `fixtures` list, so it would have existed only on this site's database, not survived a fresh install. Added a `Report` fixture filter and re-exported; confirmed captured in `fixtures/report.json`. |
| 2026-08-23 | Found & Fixed (3 real bugs, one integration test) | RFQ Stage C's real integration test of `submit_quotation()` — a feature "done" since Phase 2, never once exercised with real data — found 3 genuine, previously-invisible bugs in a single pass | **Bug 1 — missing warehouse:** identical class of failure to the RFQ-creation test-script gap above, but this time in the real production endpoint: a supplier has no way to know or provide the buyer's internal warehouse, yet ERPNext requires one per stock item row on `Supplier Quotation`. **Fixed** by auto-filling from `Item Default`'s per-company `default_warehouse` (same mechanism already proven in `proc_portal`'s New Request form), invisible to the supplier; throws a clear, actionable error (`"Item {code} has no default warehouse configured for {company}. Please contact procurement..."`) instead of ERPNext's raw validation message if genuinely none exists — verified both paths live. **Follow-up (2026-08-27):** this same clear error, not a code bug, surfaced for real on `create_rfq()` (`proc_portal/api/rfqs.py`, the identical mechanism this entry already covers) when Yasser tried creating a real RFQ for `SKU006` (Coffee Mug) — confirmed via `SELECT ... FROM tabItem Default WHERE parent='SKU006'` that it genuinely had zero rows for any company, unlike `SKU010` which already had one. Fixed as pure data, no code change: added an `Item Default` row (`company=KCSC`, `default_warehouse=Stores - K`), matching `SKU010`'s existing pattern exactly, independently reconfirmed via fresh SQL. **Bug 2 — wrong company:** fixing Bug 1 surfaced a second failure, `Warehouse Stores - K does not belong to company KCSC (Demo)` — the new `Supplier Quotation` never had its own `company` field set explicitly, so ERPNext's ambient-default resolution picked the wrong one of this site's two companies. **The exact same class of bug already found and fixed once before** in `proc_portal`'s `create_request()` (`PROC_PORTAL_SPEC.md` Known Issues, 2026-08-13) — same root cause, same fix: derive `company` explicitly (from the RFQ itself, already fetched for Bug 1's lookup) rather than trusting ambient context. **Bug 3 — quote_status never syncing:** after fixing Bugs 1-2, the Supplier Quotation was created successfully but the RFQ's `quote_status` for the acting supplier never flipped to "Received". Root-caused via ERPNext core source (`erpnext/buying/doctype/supplier_quotation/supplier_quotation.py`): the status-sync logic (`update_rfq_supplier_status()`) only runs from `on_submit`/`on_cancel` — and `submit_quotation()` only ever called `.insert()`, leaving the document permanently in Draft. This was a genuine design decision, not an obvious one-line fix (submitting on the supplier's behalf makes the document no longer editable) — confirmed with the user before implementing: `sq.submit()` added after insert, since a supplier's quotation submission is final from their side and matches ERPNext's own expected doctype lifecycle. **A 4th, separate finding, clarified as a test-methodology gap rather than a code bug:** even after all 3 fixes, `quote_status` still didn't flip on the first corrected retest — traced to the test payload never including each item's own `Request for Quotation Item` child-row `name`, which `update_rfq_supplier_status()`'s matching logic requires to link a specific quote line back to a specific RFQ line. `submit_quotation()`'s existing code already threads this through correctly (`"request_for_quotation_item": item.get("name")`) — the real `supplier_portal` frontend (`rfq_detail.html`) already reads this `name` from the RFQ's own server-rendered item data and passes it through naturally; only the ad-hoc test scripts (Stage C's original and this fix's initial retest) omitted it. Retested with the RFQ item's real row name included — `quote_status` correctly flipped to "Received". All 4 findings verified end-to-end via real HTTP + fresh SQL, not assumed; test artifacts cleaned up, `quote_status` reset to `Pending` for a clean Stage B slate. **Follow-up (2026-08-27) — the same root cause's second real consequence, now fixed:** Stage C's 3 lasting test quotations (`PUR-SQTN-2026-00005/6/7`, deliberately kept for Stage D's UI testing rather than cleaned up) turned out to carry this exact gap themselves — their `Supplier Quotation Item` rows all had `request_for_quotation_item = NULL`, since they were created by an earlier ad-hoc console script predating this test-methodology finding. Consequence: `quote_status` for all 3 real suppliers on `PUR-RFQ-2026-00003` was genuinely stuck at `Pending` (not a display bug — confirmed via fresh SQL and by loading the real `proc_portal` page as `procurement.officer.test`, checking for the literal button text `"Generate/View Comparison"` rather than the looser, initially-misleading substring `"Comparison"` which also matches the page's always-present JS method name), which correctly hid the portal's "Generate/View Comparison" button, since its Jinja gate (`rfq-detail.html`) legitimately requires at least one `Received` row. **Fixed as pure data recovery, no code change**: looked up the real RFQ Item row (`ib7s42mkmj`, the `SKU010` line on `PUR-RFQ-2026-00003`), set `request_for_quotation_item` on all 3 Supplier Quotation Items to reference it, then re-ran `update_rfq_supplier_status(1)` on each quotation — the same recovery mechanism already used once before in this same entry. Verified via fresh SQL (`quote_status` now `Received` for all 3) and by reloading the real portal page as the same test user (button text now genuinely present). Also verified `RFQCS-2026-00001`'s existing scored comparison data was unaffected throughout, not assumed: `generate_comparison_sheet()` queries `Supplier Quotation Item` by `request_for_quotation` (a different, always-correct field), never by `request_for_quotation_item`, so this gap never touched the scoring engine itself — re-confirmed identical rankings/marks (`MA Inc.` 88.0/1, `Zuckerman Security Ltd.` 68.57/2, `Summit Traders Ltd.` 53.33/3) before and after the fix. |
| 2026-08-23 | Found, Not a Bug | RFQ Approval workflow's first live test failed twice before succeeding — both real ERPNext validation requirements specific to `Request for Quotation`, not workflow/permission problems | First attempt: `ValidationError: Row #1: Warehouse is mandatory for stock Item SKU010` — `erpnext.buying.utils.validate_stock_item_warehouse()` requires a `warehouse` per item row for any stock item, a stricter rule than Material Request's item warehouse field (confirmed earlier this project to be genuinely optional at the ERPNext core level). Second attempt (after adding `warehouse`): `ValidationError: Row #1: UOM Conversion Factor is mandatory` — `BuyingController.set_qty_as_per_stock_uom()` requires an explicit `uom` (with a resolvable `conversion_factor`) per item row. Both are real, pre-existing ERPNext constraints on RFQ Item rows, unrelated to anything built today — confirmed by the traceback originating entirely in `erpnext/buying/utils.py`/`erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`, not any of this project's own code. Neither failed insert was actually persisted (confirmed via fresh SQL both times — `bench console`'s continue-past-exception behavior meant later cells printed a pre-allocated `doc.name` as if creation had succeeded, another instance of the documented console-testing gotcha). Fixed the *test script*, not the doctype/workflow: the third attempt included `warehouse`, `uom`, and `conversion_factor` explicitly and succeeded end-to-end. **General lesson for any future RFQ-creation code (including proc_portal's upcoming creation screens):** item rows need `warehouse` and `uom`/`conversion_factor` set explicitly — don't assume Material Request's more permissive item-row rules carry over to RFQ. |
| 2026-08-23 | Confirmed Acceptable | Department Officer + Stock User can create Material Requests | Found via live permission testing in `proc_portal`: any user holding both Department Officer (our custom role, review/approve only by design) and ERPNext's native Stock User role (granted separately for the Issue-from-Stock flow) can ALSO create Material Requests, since Stock User natively has `create=1` on Material Request (a pre-existing OOB grant, not something this project added). Permissions are additive across all of a user's roles, so this combination genuinely allows creation, deviating from the original Department Officer role design (review/approve only). Confirmed with Yasser (2026-08-23): this is acceptable, reasonable real-world behavior — warehouse/stock staff plausibly do need to raise their own material requests too. Not tightened. Documented so this isn't mistaken for an oversight in future review. |
| 2026-08-22 | Deliberate Scope Decision | Purchase Order Amendment approval has no automated effect | Confirmed via hands-on desk testing that reaching "Approved" state is purely a documentation/internal-approval record — it does not update the linked Purchase Order's actual fields, and does not notify the supplier. This was flagged directly to Yasser during testing; decision made to leave as documentation-only for now rather than build automation ahead of real, workshop-confirmed business rules for exactly how amendments should apply. Future work: (1) auto-update PO fields matching the amendment's description once approved, (2) trigger a supplier notification (ties into the still-unbuilt Notification Master List items documented in `PROC_SUITE_ARCHITECTURE.md`). |
| 2026-08-22 | Found & Fixed | "Insufficient permission for Purchase Order" surfaced testing the new Purchase Order Amendment form — Procurement Officer had no permission to select a Purchase Order on the form's `purchase_order` link field | Scoped specifically to the PO Amendment feature — Material Request approval never needed Procurement Officer to read Purchase Order directly. Confirmed no existing `Custom DocPerm` covered this combination beforehand (ruling out something else masking the real cause). Fixed by extending `setup_po_amendment_permissions()` in `setup.py` with an idempotent check-before-create grant of `read=1` on `Purchase Order` for `Procurement Officer`, alongside the PO Amendment doctype's own permissions in the same function — consistent with the file's existing pattern rather than adding a separate function for a single permission. Verified via `Custom DocPerm` query and a clean `migrate` (exit 0, no orphans). |
| 2026-08-14 | Found & Fixed | "Insufficient permission for Price List" surfaced for the first time testing the actual desk New Material Request form | The desk form renders every field's dropdown directly, including `Price List` (used internally for item rate lookups) — something portal/API testing never exercises, since `proc_portal`'s own New Request form doesn't render or need a Price List field at all. Confirms an expected pattern, not a one-off: the desk UI surfaces supporting-doctype permission gaps that portal/API testing structurally cannot, since it renders every linked field's dropdown, not just the fields our own code actually touches. Fixed by adding `"Price List"` to the existing `SUPPORTING_DOCTYPES` list in `setup.py` (same read-only, 4-role pattern already covering Item/Item Group/UOM/Warehouse/Brand/Company/Department) rather than a one-off fix — reproducible on any future install via `after_migrate()`. Verified: all 4 `SUPPORTING_ROLES` now have `read=1` on `Price List`; the pre-existing native-role core `DocPerm` entries (`Purchase User`, `Sales User`, `Sales Master Manager`, `Manufacturing User`, `Purchase Master Manager`) were confirmed unaffected (same Custom-DocPerm-mirroring behavior already documented for the Stock Settings fix, checked again rather than assumed). |
| 2026-08-14 | Found & Fixed | `operations.officer.test` hit "No permission for Stock Settings" creating a Stock Entry from an "Approved - Issue" Material Request, found via hands-on desk testing | A genuine ERPNext OOB gap, not a lost role or a broken custom permission — confirmed methodically before fixing. `frappe.get_roles()` confirmed `Stock User` was still present (ruling out role loss). Core `tabDocPerm` for `Stock Settings` grants read only to `Sales User` and `Stock Manager` — **not** `Stock User`, even though Stock Entry's creation flow reads Stock Settings internally for defaults (negative-stock allowance, price-list-rate behavior, etc.). No `Custom DocPerm` existed for `Stock Settings` at all beforehand, confirming this predates any of our own permission work. **Fixed narrowly**: granted `read=1` on `Stock Settings` to `Department Officer` only, via a new `setup_stock_settings_permission()` function in `setup.py` (same idempotent pattern as every other permission fix in this file), rather than modifying the native `Stock User` role globally — avoids any unaudited system-wide effect on other `Stock User` holders outside this app's scope. One side effect worth recording, not a problem: adding the first `Custom DocPerm` for a doctype makes Frappe use `Custom DocPerm` exclusively for that doctype going forward, so Frappe automatically mirrored the pre-existing `Sales User`/`Stock Manager` core permissions into `Custom DocPerm` too — confirmed their original core `DocPerm` rows are untouched and their effective access is unchanged, only `Department Officer` gained anything new. |
| 2026-08-14 | Found & Fixed | Creating the `Purchase Order Amendment` DocType via the Document API failed with `ValidationError: Cannot set Assign Submit if not Submittable` | The DocType dict included a permissions entry with `"submit": 1`, but didn't set `"is_submittable": 1` on the DocType itself — required for any doctype using Submit/Cancel/docstatus workflow transitions. Frappe correctly rejected the insert before persisting anything (confirmed via a fresh DB check — zero rows created). Fixed by adding `"is_submittable": 1` to the DocType dict; retry succeeded. **General lesson:** any workflow-driven custom DocType created via the Document API (not the Desk UI, which sets this automatically when you tick "Is Submittable") needs `is_submittable: 1` set explicitly. |
| 2026-08-14 | Found, Not Fixed (out of scope) | 2 pre-existing negative stock quantity anomalies found on this dev site during Stock Management testing, unrelated to anything being tested | Not investigated further or fixed — flagged here so a future session doesn't rediscover this from scratch. Needs its own investigation before considered a real issue vs. dev-data artifact. |
| 2026-08-14 | Process Lesson | bench console testing pitfalls found during Stock Management verification | (1) Writes made in a bench console session are NOT automatically committed — if the session exits (or a script ends) before an explicit `frappe.db.commit()`, those writes silently vanish, which can make a test look like it failed when it actually just wasn't persisted. Always call `frappe.db.commit()` explicitly before relying on a write's effects in a later step. (2) A cleanup script that calls `doc.cancel()` then unconditionally proceeds to force-delete, without checking whether `cancel()` actually succeeded, can end up force-deleting a document that's still genuinely submitted (docstatus=1) if `cancel()` silently failed or was skipped — always verify `cancel()` succeeded (check docstatus afterward) before force-deleting, especially for documents with real linked stock/ledger impact. |

Since the app was still an empty shell, executed as uninstall + folder removal + fresh `bench new-app proc_app`, rather than an in-place rename — avoids any risk of leftover references. |

| 2026-08-14 | Found & Fixed (two rounds) | `bench migrate` silently deleted the 3 just-moved doctypes as "orphans" (exit code 0, no error text) | A new Module Def ("Proc App") was created in the DB without adding it to `apps/proc_app/proc_app/modules.txt` first — migrate's doctype-discovery scan never recognized the moved files as belonging to a valid module, and its orphan-cleanup pass deleted the now-unreachable `tabDocType` records for all 3 doctypes (underlying DB tables were never dropped, confirmed via `SHOW TABLES`; 0 rows existed in any of them anyway, so no data was actually lost). Caught by explicitly checking the migrate log's "Removing orphan doctypes" section, not just grepping for the literal words "Error"/"Traceback"/"Failed" — an exit code of 0 does not mean nothing significant happened. **First fix** (adding `Proc App` to `modules.txt`) was necessary but not sufficient: a second migrate still didn't register the doctypes, and `proc_app` didn't even appear in migrate's "Updating DocTypes for X" sync list at all. **Second fix**, root-caused via `frappe.scrub()`/`get_module_path()` rather than guessed: module "Proc App" scrubs to `proc_app`, and Frappe expects that module's doctypes at `apps/proc_app/proc_app/proc_app/doctype/...` (a third nesting level, mirroring `supplier_portal/supplier_portal/supplier_portal/`'s existing pattern) — the files had been placed one level shallower. Moved them one level deeper to match. A third migrate then correctly registered all 3 doctypes under module "Proc App" with zero orphans, confirmed via `tabDocType` query and a real functional smoke test (create + delete) for both `Supplier ASN` and `Supplier Invoice Dispute`. **General lesson:** when creating a new Module Def for an app, always add it to that app's `modules.txt` in the same step, and remember that a module's expected doctype folder path is `get_module_path(module_name)` — i.e., `<app_package>/<scrub(module_name)>/doctype/`, not necessarily matching whatever nesting depth was assumed by analogy to a different app. |

| 1.104 | 2026-09-12 | KCSC | **Fixed the two translation collisions flagged in v1.103 (`Address`/`Title`, `Contact`/`Contact Person`) using context-scoped `Translation` records**, since neither could be resolved by a global rename without moving the collision to the other member of the pair — `Purchase Invoice`, `Purchase Receipt`, and `Supplier Quotation` all carry every one of the four fields on the same form. Investigated whether `Translation.context` actually does anything for field labels before proposing it, rather than assuming: confirmed `context = doctype name` (`df.parent`) is Frappe's own existing convention (`frappe/model/document.py`/`base_document.py`'s validation messages), and traced it all the way to the desk form-rendering path itself (`base_input.js`'s `set_label()`, `translate.js`'s `frappe._`, fed by the same `get_all_translations()` merged dict at boot) — not just the Python side. Proved it empirically at both levels: a throwaway `_()` context test (created and removed before touching anything real), and a real rendered desk form — `address_display`/`contact_display` are hidden by Frappe's own client-side "don't show an empty computed field" behavior and no document on this site had real address/contact data, so a test document's fields were given temporary placeholder values, the "Address & Contact" tab was opened in a real headless-Chromium session (reusing the `dpkg-deb`-extracted-library technique from the RTL work, since this sandbox has no system browser or passwordless `sudo`), the three labels read as genuinely distinct on the same tab, and the placeholders were reverted immediately after. Shipped `Address` → `عنوان المورد` and `Contact` → `معلومات الاتصال`, scoped to the three affected doctypes (6 records), leaving `Title`/`Contact Person` on their already-correct global translations — bringing the total to 438. Re-ran the collision scan: unchanged at the global level (expected, by design) and confirmed both pairs now distinct wherever the doctype context is passed. Extending the fixture needed more than a bigger list this time: a context-scoped record shares its `source_text` with an existing global entry, so `hooks.py`'s single `Translation` fixture entry now combines its existing `filters` (language/contributed) with a new `or_filters` (the 432-string list OR a match on `context` in the three doctypes) — confirmed matching exactly 438 via `frappe.get_all()` before writing it, and deliberately not a second fixture entry for the same doctype, since `export_fixtures()` names its output file purely from the doctype and a second entry would silently overwrite the first's `translation.json` rather than adding to it (confirmed by reading `frappe/utils/fixtures.py`). Verified live on `deploytest.local`: all 438 records arrive and resolve correctly. The same `doctype.json` accidental-sweep noise recurred a fourth time on `export-fixtures` and was reverted before committing, as every time before. |
| 1.103 | 2026-09-11 | KCSC | **Added 334 hand-reviewed semantic corrections to already-translated desk strings** (Section 2.11), the largest of the three Translation-override rounds, bringing the fixture total to 432. Regression-checked the widest, most generic vocabulary yet (`Quantity`, `Warehouse`, `Item`, `Rate`, `Date`, `Description`, `Company`, `Project`, `Reference`, `Received`, `Completed`, `Open`, `Other`, `Total`) across both `DocField` and `Custom Field` site-wide — all read correctly everywhere they're used. Re-ran the collision scan and found two that need real, reported attention (not filed as harmless): `Address`/`Title` and `Contact`/`Contact Person` both collide on `Purchase Invoice`, `Purchase Receipt`, and `Supplier Quotation` — three of our own doctypes carrying both fields under the identical Arabic label. Neither fixed in this pass (a context-scoped `Translation` record is the likely remedy, a decision for a future task); everything else re-confirmed as the same harmless near-synonym/spelling-variant class as the prior two rounds. `hooks.py`'s explicit `source_text` list extended from 98 to 432 names. Verified live on `deploytest.local`: all 432 arrive via `bench migrate` and resolve correctly, with every earlier correction (`Amount`, `Draft`, `Quantity`, `Website`, etc.) still intact. The same accidental-fixture-sweep risk from `export-fixtures` recurred a third time (`doctype.json` bookkeeping noise) and was caught and reverted before committing, as before. |

Built accordingly: the structural cart path carries no colour at all; only the two wheel "solid dot" circles use `fill="var(--icon-stroke)"`, matching frappe's own `timeless/icons.svg` convention for that exact case. The KCSC sky blue was deliberately NOT hardcoded anywhere. New sprite at `proc_app/public/icons/proc_icons.svg` (one symbol, `icon-kcsc-procurement`), registered via `app_include_icons` in `hooks.py`. The earlier MR-only formula had reconciled correctly by pure coincidence every previous time (`ordered_amount`/`actual_expense` were always 0 in those tests) — this is exactly the silent-mismatch failure mode the whole feature exists to prevent, only surfaced once a second doctype's own bucket held a genuinely independent, nonzero value. **Fixed**: `remaining_before`/`remaining_after` are now computed from the previewed doctype's own bucket only (`requested_amount` for MR, `ordered_amount` for PO, `actual_expense` for PI); the other two buckets are still returned for context but never summed into the same figure; the optional cumulative check is also evaluated when a Budget has it enabled, taking whichever of the two (own-bucket vs. cumulative) is more severe. **A third, real discrepancy found the same way, this time for Purchase Invoice specifically**: `get_item_details()` (used by the preview) and `Purchase Invoice.set_expense_account()` (the actual controller method that runs at real validate time — confirmed via source, Material Request/Purchase Order have no equivalent dedicated method) are genuinely different mechanisms; the latter unconditionally overrides a stock item's `expense_account` to the company's own "Stock Received But Not Billed" account whenever perpetual inventory is enabled, `is_opening` is "No", it's not a fixed asset, and there's no linked Purchase Receipt — confirmed live: an explicitly-set `5208` account was silently overridden to `2210 - Stock Received But Not Billed - K` on a genuine, submitted Purchase Invoice. Now compares against `doc.get_doc_before_save()` and only overwrites all rows when `before.get("set_cost_center") != doc.set_cost_center` — chosen over the alternative (leave server-side as fill-blanks-only, let the Client Script handle the overwrite) because it makes the behavior consistent whether the document is created via desk, portal, or any other API path, not only when someone happens to use the desk form. **The new-document edge case flagged up front was real and confirmed via source, not assumed**: `get_doc_before_save()` returns `None` for a brand-new document — traced through `frappe/model/document.py`'s `load_doc_before_save()`, which returns immediately (leaving `_doc_before_save` unset) when `self.is_new()` is true, called from `check_if_latest()` before `before_validate` ever fires, for both `.insert()` and `.save()`. Handled explicitly: `before is None` counts as "changed", so a new document setting `set_cost_center` still applies to every row, matching the Client Script's own behavior for a freshly-filled-in form. **The portal's New Request form needed no change, verified rather than assumed**: `create_request()` only ever creates a document once, applying `cost_center` to each item row directly at creation (`row["cost_center"] = cost_center`) — there is no "master field" on an existing, editable document the way desk now has, so the whole overwrite-vs-preserve distinction doesn't apply there. **Live-tested all three required scenarios in one sequence on a single document**: (1) setting `set_cost_center` on a new document — both rows correctly got `IT - K`; (2) editing one row's `cost_center` to `Operations - K` and saving *without* changing `set_cost_center` — that row correctly survived as `Operations - K`, the untouched row stayed `IT - K` (the critical case this whole change exists for); (3) then genuinely changing `set_cost_center` to `Main - K` and saving again — *both* rows, including the previously-edited one, were correctly overwritten to `Main - K`. Test document deleted afterward. No `hooks.py` change this time (only the function body inside `material_request_hooks.py` and the Client Script's own content) — confirmed via process start time that werkzeug's file-watcher had already auto-restarted the worker before the live test ran, so no manual restart was needed, unlike prior `hooks.py`-structure changes. |

| 1.79 | 2026-09-04 | KCSC | **Fixed a real cross-company bug found on Yasser's own desk test**: a Material Request created with `company="KCSC (Demo)"` but `requesting_department="Accounts - K"` (a `KCSC` department — a genuine, pre-existing data mismatch on that document) threw `"Cost Center: Main - KD does not belong to the Company: KCSC"` when its Forward-to-Purchase spawn ran. **Root-caused precisely, not assumed**: the spawn hook (`on_material_request_update()`) derived the new document's `company` via `frappe.db.get_value("Department", doc.requesting_department, "company")` rather than copying `doc.company` — so it picked up the *department's* company (`KCSC`) while the copied item rows still carried the *source document's* cost centers (`KCSC (Demo)`-scoped, e.g. `Main - KD`), tripping ERPNext's own `AccountsController.validate_company()` (`accounts_controller.py:551-565`) the instant the spawned document tried to save. **Fix 1 — `company = doc.company`** (a bare copy, not a derive-if-empty fallback): confirmed `Material Request.company` is mandatory (`reqd=1`) and this hook only ever runs on an already-validated, already-saved source document (`on_update` fires post-save) — it cannot genuinely be empty here, so a fallback would guard against an impossible case. Documented at length, in the code itself, exactly why this must not be "helpfully" reverted back to a department lookup: the department-derivation this replaces was itself an earlier fix (v1.36) for a *different* problem (the spawn never set `company` at all, silently falling to an ambient session default) — deriving from the department was *one* way to plug that gap, but the real fix for v1.36's underlying scenario is Fix 2, not this line. **Fix 2 — reject the mismatch at the source**: new `validate_department_company(doc, method)` in `material_request_hooks.py`, hooked on `before_validate` (alongside the existing `propagate_cost_center`, both now registered as a list under that same event key), checks both `requesting_department` and `concerned_department` against `doc.company` and throws a specific, actionable error (`"{label} '{department}' belongs to company '{dept_company}', but this request is for '{doc.company}'. Please select a department from the correct company."`) if either department's own company doesn't match — the real fix, since it stops a mismatched request from ever being created, on any path (desk, API, or anything else that doesn't go through `proc_portal`, which has its own equivalent check — see `PROC_PORTAL_SPEC.md`). **Confirmed the real `Department.company` data first**: of 27 departments, only the root "All Departments" group node has a blank company — every real, selectable leaf department has one — so a blank is treated as "any company" (skipped), never itself flagged as invalid. **Also added desk-side filtering** (Client Script "Material Request Department Company Filter", added to the fixture list): `frm.set_query` on both `requesting_department` and `concerned_department` restricted to `{company: doc.company, is_group: 0}` — prevents the invalid choice in the UI, though `validate_department_company()` is the actual enforcement (the portal's own New Request form already had the equivalent client-side filter, `new.html`'s `filteredRequestingDepartments`/`filteredDepartments`, from earlier work — nothing to add there). This being a `hooks.py` change, required a full process restart (not just `clear-cache`) to take effect, per the standing project finding. **Live-tested all three paths**: (1) the exact cross-company combination (`KCSC (Demo)` doc, `Accounts - K` requesting department) — correctly rejected with the precise message: `"Requesting Department 'Accounts - K' belongs to company 'KCSC', but this request is for 'KCSC (Demo)'. Please select a department from the correct company."`; (2) a same-company `KCSC (Demo)` request (`Accounts - KD`/`Operations - KD`) — created normally, then taken through the real 3-step approval chain (`Submit` → `Approve` → `Approve - Forward to Purchase`) as the genuine role-holding test users, via real `apply_workflow()` calls (not a bypass) — completed with no exception; the spawned document (`MAT-MR-2026-00039`) genuinely inherited `company: "KCSC (Demo)"`, and its copied item row (`cost_center: "Main - KD"`) matched correctly, with zero mismatch. Both test documents (source cancelled then deleted; spawn, still Draft, deleted directly) cleaned up afterward. |
| 1.78 | 2026-09-04 | KCSC | **Added a document-level "Cost Center" field to Material Request** (`set_cost_center`, Custom Field, inserted after the native `set_warehouse`) that fills item rows — mirroring ERPNext's own `set_warehouse` pattern, deliberately not copying it exactly. **Traced the real `set_warehouse` mechanism first** rather than assuming: it's a two-part, asymmetric mechanism, not a single propagation function. (1) On the `set_warehouse` field itself changing, the JS controller's `autofill_warehouse()` (`erpnext/public/js/controllers/transaction.js`) **unconditionally overwrites every item row**, no blank-check at all. (2) On a *new* row being added (`items_add`, same file), the native handler fills that one row's warehouse *only if it's currently blank*. (3) Separately, server-side, `reset_default_field_value()` (`erpnext/utilities/transaction_base.py`, called from `Material Request.validate()`) runs in the opposite direction — if the item rows end up with more than one distinct warehouse value, it *clears* `set_warehouse` back to blank, so the header never shows a misleadingly uniform value; it is not a propagation mechanism at all. **Deliberately deviated from (1)**: this task's own instructions flagged as CRITICAL that propagation must not clobber a row where a different cost center was deliberately set, and cost center drives budget checking (a compliance-relevant field), unlike warehouse (routing convenience) — so both the Client Script and the server-side hook fill blank rows only, never overwrite a set value. Matched (2) exactly, since it was already blank-only in the native behavior and doesn't conflict with the no-clobber requirement. **Implementation**: `Material Request-set_cost_center` Custom Field (Link, options "Cost Center", `insert_after: set_warehouse`), added to `hooks.py`'s existing explicit-name Custom Field fixture list (not broadening its filter). A new Client Script, "Material Request Set Cost Center" (also added to the fixture list), provides (a) a `frm.set_query` on `set_cost_center` filtering to `{company: doc.company, is_group: 0}` — the exact same filter shape already used for `set_warehouse`/`set_from_warehouse` on this doctype — (b) a `set_cost_center(frm)` trigger that fills blank item rows on change, and (c) a `Material Request Item` `items_add` trigger filling a newly-added blank row, mirroring native behavior (2). **Server-side safety net**: `material_request_hooks.py` gained `propagate_cost_center(doc, method)`, so an API- or import-created document (where the Client Script never runs) behaves identically. **Found and fixed a real ordering bug while live-testing**: registering this on the `validate` doc event initially did nothing — `Material Request(BuyingController).validate()` calls `super().validate()` first, which runs `AccountsController.set_missing_item_details()` and fills any blank `cost_center` with the Company-level fallback (`Main - K`) *before* a `doc_events`-registered `validate` hook ever runs (confirmed: Frappe composes the class's own `validate()` and `doc_events` hooks as one chain, class method first) — so by the time the hook checked `if not item.cost_center`, it was already non-blank and never got the intended value. Fixed by moving the hook to `before_validate`, which fires ahead of `self.validate()` entirely, so it fills the blank rows before `BuyingController`'s own fallback logic ever sees them. This being a `hooks.py` change, a full process restart was required to take effect (not just `clear-cache` — same documented requirement as `PROC_PORTAL_SPEC.md` v1.9). **Live-tested end to end** after the fix: created a Purchase-type Material Request with `set_cost_center: "IT - K"` and two item rows with no `cost_center` of their own — both rows correctly resolved to `IT - K` on insert. Then explicitly set one row's `cost_center` to `"Operations - K"` and re-saved: that row's override survived (stayed `Operations - K`), while the untouched second row correctly remained `IT - K` — confirming the fill-blanks, no-clobber behavior genuinely holds across a second save, not just the initial insert. Test document deleted afterward. |

| 1.75 | 2026-09-04 | KCSC | **Budget demo scenario for the bank client presentation** — real cost centers, real budgets, and a live-verified enforcement test, on desk, ahead of any portal integration. Not application code; documented here for the record since it's the basis of a live demo Yasser will run. **Structure created**: 2 new leaf Cost Centers under `KCSC - K` — `IT - K` and `Operations - K` (no suitable ones existed; only `Main - K` did). 3 submitted Budgets (`docstatus: 1`, confirmed via a fresh DB read, not just the in-memory doc after insert): `IT - K` / `5201 - Administrative Expenses - K` / 50,000; `IT - K` / `5208 - Office Maintenance Expenses - K` / 30,000; `Operations - K` / `5211 - Print and Stationery - K` / 20,000 — all against FY `2026` (the only fiscal year on this site), `distribution_frequency: Monthly`, `distribute_equally: 1`. **A real gap, flagged rather than papered over**: this site has no IT-specific chart-of-accounts entries at all (no Software/Hardware/Consumables) — the 3 accounts above are the closest genuine equivalents in KCSC's actual CoA, not literal matches; worth raising with Yasser before the live demo in case the bank expects to see accounts named for what they actually are. **The real v16 Budget schema differs from what a pre-v16 mental model would assume, confirmed via `get_meta()` before building anything**: one Budget document per (cost_center, account) pair with single `account`/`budget_amount` fields (not a multi-row grid); each applicable-on category has *two* action fields (`..._on_mr`/`..._on_po` for annual vs. accumulated-monthly), not one; no `fiscal_year`/`monthly_distribution` link fields — replaced by `from_fiscal_year`/`to_fiscal_year` and `distribution_frequency`/`distribute_equally` directly on the doctype. Applied as instructed: MR — `Warn`/`Warn` (annual/monthly); PO — `Stop`/`Stop`; booking actual expenses — `Warn`/`Warn`. `use_legacy_budget_controller` confirmed `0` (the new `BudgetValidation` engine, per the earlier read-only Budget investigation) and `Company.exception_budget_approver_role` confirmed unset — a "Stop" today is a genuine hard block with no configured override/escalation path; worth knowing before the demo in case the bank asks "what happens when someone needs an exception."<br><br>**A real bug in the verification script itself, caught before it produced a false, alarming conclusion — not a flaw in ERPNext's enforcement**: the first attempt wrapped `doc.submit()` in `try/except` but called `frappe.db.commit()` *unconditionally* afterward, regardless of whether the exception was caught. `validate_budget()` for Purchase Order runs from `on_submit()` (confirmed in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py:455`, inside `on_submit()`, not `validate()`) — meaning Frappe's core `_submit()` had already flipped `docstatus` to 1 and written it *before* the budget check even ran, and the unconditional commit afterward persisted that write despite the caught `BudgetError`, genuinely leaving a budget-breaching PO submitted. Confirmed via a fresh, separate console session (not trusting the same-session read) that `docstatus` was really `1`. **Not a real enforcement flaw**: in genuine desk/browser usage, Frappe wraps the whole HTTP request in one transaction and rolls back everything on any unhandled exception — no special handling needed there. The fix for the *test script* was calling `frappe.db.rollback()` instead of `commit()` on the caught exception, mirroring what the real request-transaction wrapper already does automatically. Re-verified cleanly afterward, confirmed via fresh reads both times. **The real, verified exception message** (what the bank will actually see): *"Annual Budget for Account **5208 - Office Maintenance Expenses - K** against **Cost Center**: **IT - K** is **د.ا 30,000.00**. It will be exceeded by **د.ا 5,000.00**"* — clear, specific (names the exact account/cost-center/budget/overage), not cryptic; genuinely demo-ready as-is. **Passing case** (10,000 against the same 30,000 budget) submitted cleanly, `docstatus: 1`, confirmed via a fresh read. **Cleanup**: all 4 test Purchase Orders created during verification (including the 2 wrongly-left-submitted ones from the script bug) confirmed to have zero GL Entries/Stock Ledger Entries (a Purchase Order posts neither on its own), cancelled where needed and hard-deleted by exact name; independently reconfirmed gone, and the 3 demo budgets confirmed still clean (no leftover committed exposure) afterward. **Fixture-tracking, flagged rather than assumed**: none of this — Cost Centers, Budgets, or the demo amounts — is application logic; it's live, site-specific business/demo data, the same category this project has consistently kept out of fixtures throughout (e.g. real Department/Warehouse *records* were never fixture-tracked, only Custom Fields added to those doctypes were). Recommending **no fixture-tracking and no commit of any schema/hooks change**, since none was made — this Changelog entry is the only record of what exists on this site. A short walkthrough for Yasser: create/attempt a PO on `IT - K` against `5208 - Office Maintenance Expenses - K` for more than 30,000 (any leaf item, any supplier, company `KCSC`) — submission is genuinely blocked with the message above; the same PO at 10,000 submits cleanly; a Material Request against the same cost center/account only *warns*, never blocks, showing the MR→PO escalation the bank's own approval chain likely mirrors. |
| 1.74 | 2026-09-04 | KCSC | Drill-down links on the 6 `get_dashboard_summary()` stat cards (Procurement Dashboard, v1.73) — clicking a card's number opens the desk list view pre-filtered to the exact same document set the metric counts. **Confirmed each metric's real criteria from the actual query before mapping anything, not assumed from the card label**: Open Requests — Material Request, `docstatus != 2` AND `workflow_state NOT IN [...]`; Active RFQs — Request for Quotation, `workflow_state = "Approved"` AND `docstatus = 1`; POs This Month — Purchase Order, `docstatus = 1` AND `transaction_date >= <first of this month>`, deliberately **no upper bound** (matching the metric's own raw SQL exactly, even though that means future-dated POs this month are included too — the drill-down must show the same set the card counted, not a "more sensible" one); Contracts Expiring — Contract, `status = "Active"` AND `end_date` within `[today, today+30]` inclusive; Unbilled POs — Purchase Order, `docstatus = 1` AND `per_billed < 100`; Awaiting Payment — Purchase Invoice, `docstatus = 1` AND `outstanding_amount > 0`. **Operator support for `set_route('List', ...)` verified by tracing real v16 source end to end, not assumed from a simple equality example**: `frappe/public/js/frappe/router.js`'s `set_route()` assigns a plain-object 3rd argument directly to `frappe.route_options` (`if ($.isPlainObject(route[2])) { frappe.route_options = route[2]; }`), then JSON-encodes each value into the URL's query string; `frappe/public/js/frappe/list/list_view.js`'s `parse_filters_from_route_options()` reads it back, and for a 2-element array value produces `[doctype, field, value[0], value[1]]` — genuine operator support (`!=`, `not in`, `<`, `>`, `>=`), confirmed directly in the parser's own branches, not inferred from a working example alone. The one genuinely non-obvious case — Contracts Expiring needing two conditions (`>=`/`<=`) on the *same* field `end_date` — is also natively supported: an array-of-arrays value (`end_date: [['>=', a], ['<=', b]]`) makes the same parser push two separate filter tuples for that field, which the list view's filter model ANDs together correctly (confirmed in the parser's own array-of-arrays branch, then independently re-verified by replicating the exact parsing logic against all 6 real payloads in an isolated Node script — every payload resolved to the intended tuple set, including Contracts Expiring's two `end_date` tuples). No card needed a fallback or a dropped drill-down — every operator this task needed is genuinely supported. **Implementation**: `stat_card()` gained an optional `drilldown` parameter (`{doctype, filters}`); when present, a new `.dashboard-stat-card-link` class plus a `data-drilldown` attribute holding `esc(JSON.stringify(drilldown))` — the same JSON-in-HTML-attribute safety rule already established for `js_attr()`, applied here to a plain data-attribute rather than an Alpine expression, since this is a delegated click handler (`$(document).on('click', '.dashboard-stat-card-link', ...)`), matching `rfq_comparison.js`'s own established `.rfq-po-link` pattern exactly rather than introducing a new wiring style.
