Ported the full page (9 stat cards across two grids, 10 charts across two grids, including the "Contract Portfolio" section) rather than a partial "6 stat cards + 8 charts" subset — that undercount only covered the main section; the full page is what's genuinely there and what all 10 `proc_portal.api.dashboard.*` endpoints are for. **A real platform constraint hit mid-build, not worked around silently**: `Page.autoname()` hard-truncates any page name to 20 characters (`frappe/core/doctype/page/page.py:46`, `self.name = ...[:20]`) — `"procurement-dashboard"` (21 chars) silently became `"procurement-dashboar"` (missing the final "d") on first insert, creating a mismatch against the already-created `procurement_dashboard` folder/file. Caught before building further, the bad record deleted, and the route/folder/file renamed to `procurement-dash` (17 chars) throughout — the CSS class name itself (`.procurement-dashboard-page`) was kept fully descriptive, since only the `Page.name`/route is actually constrained, not arbitrary CSS identifiers.<br><br>**A real bug found and fixed during the port, not assumed absent**: the portal's own `dashboard.html` writes `<div x-data="dashboardPage()" x-init="init()">` — but Alpine's own `x-data` directive handler *already* auto-calls any `init()` method the data object defines (confirmed directly in the real, fetched `alpinejs@3.17.1` bundle's own minified source: `t.hasOwnProperty("init") && typeof t.init === "function" && data.init()`, run as part of processing `x-data` itself). The explicit `x-init="init()"` on top of that calls `init()` a **second** time — confirmed via an isolated, minimal jsdom+Alpine reproduction (bare `x-data`+`x-init`, no dashboard markup at all) before concluding it was real, not a fluke of this page's complexity. This means the **portal's own dashboard has always fetched all 10 API endpoints twice and built all 10 charts twice on every page load** — silently self-healing there only because its own per-canvas `Chart.getChart(id)+destroy()` guards happen to destroy-and-recreate the first pass's charts when the second pass runs against the same still-live canvases, at the cost of a wasted, invisible duplicate round-trip. Not in scope to fix the portal itself here, but worth knowing. Our port drops the redundant `x-init="init()"` entirely (Alpine's own auto-call is sufficient) and, once that's fixed, the portal's own per-canvas `Chart.getChart(id)` guards become provably dead code (by the time they'd run, every canvas is always freshly created by the registry-based destroy described below, so the lookup can never find anything) — removed rather than kept for "fidelity," since keeping demonstrably-unreachable guards would be misleading, not safe.<br><br>**The canvas-reuse/destroy-guard risk flagged for this task was real, and needed a different fix than the literal instruction implied**: `Chart.getChart(id)` resolves by re-querying `document.getElementById(id)` — after `container.html()` replaces the DOM, that id belongs to a brand-new, chart-less canvas, so a post-replace lookup can only ever miss the prior instance, leaking it. Fixed with a page-level `proc_app.procurement_dashboard.charts` array: every `new Chart(...)` is wrapped in `register_chart()`, and `render()` destroys every stored instance by reference *before* `container.html()` runs (mirroring the exact same before-not-after ordering `rfq_comparison.js` already uses for `Alpine.destroyTree()`), so destruction never depends on DOM-element identity surviving a replace. Both grids reuse `portal.css`'s own real `.dashboard-stat-grid`/`.dashboard-chart-grid` classes verbatim (confirmed genuine `display: grid` rules, not invented) — no inline `display` was ever added alongside their `x-show` bindings, avoiding the v1.72 class of bug by construction, not by discipline alone. `buildLegend()` now runs `item.label` through the established `esc()` helper before interpolating into `innerHTML` — the portal's own version doesn't, and `item.label` is live DB data (supplier/status/category names); a small, deliberate hardening applied during the port, not a redesign.<br><br>**Sidebar wiring**: added a "Dashboard" entry to both `Workspace.links` (right after the "Procurement" `Card Break`, `link_type: Page`) and `Workspace Sidebar.items` (right after "Home", `child: 0` — a genuine top-level item, not swept into the "Reports" group, respecting the order-dependence finding from v1.72), icon `layout-dashboard` (verified present in the merged sprite pool before use). **A real bookkeeping bug hit and fixed while inserting these**: building the new child-table lists via `l.as_dict()` without stripping the existing rows' own baked-in `idx` produced two rows sharing `idx=2` on the first attempt, and a second, careless re-run (against a list that already contained the first attempt's persisted "Dashboard" row) produced two literal duplicate "Dashboard" rows — both caught by re-fetching fresh and inspecting before moving on, both fixed by explicitly de-duplicating and renumbering `idx` sequentially by list position rather than trusting stale per-row values.<br><br>**The task's own premise about a pre-existing verification harness was checked and found false, not assumed true**: no jsdom+Alpine+Chart.js+node-canvas harness existed anywhere on this system from any prior dashboard work — built one fresh (`canvas`/`chart.js`/`jsdom` installed locally in the scratchpad), which surfaced real jsdom/Node environment gaps needing standard polyfills (`ResizeObserver`, `requestAnimationFrame`/`cancelAnimationFrame` on Node's own `global`, not just `window`, since Chart.js loads via a plain `require()`) — none of which are page bugs, all confirmed as known Chart.js-under-jsdom testing gaps before working around them. **Verified live and at runtime**: all 10 `proc_portal.api.dashboard.*` endpoints return `200`; `frappe.desk.desk_page.getpage` delivers the script with the redundant `x-init` genuinely absent from the actual markup line (confirmed precisely — a naive substring check first flagged a false positive, matching text in this very file's own explanatory comments, not the shipped HTML); the corrected jsdom+real-Alpine+real-Chart.js harness confirms exactly one `init()`/component-factory call per render cycle (not two), all 10 charts genuinely attach (`Chart.getChart(id)` resolves for every canvas), calling the full render cycle twice in the same persistent context leaves exactly 10 chart instances (not 20 — proving the registry-based destroy-before-replace works, the scenario that matters most in desk, where `refresh()` re-runs `render()` repeatedly without ever reloading the page), and both stat/chart grids resolve with no inline `display` at all once loading completes (class-only, `x-show`-safe). |

"Page" added to `hooks.py`'s fixtures (confirmed empirically, not assumed, that `bench export-fixtures` genuinely supports it as a fixture doctype, producing a real `fixtures/page.json`) — matching this project's established convention of fixture-tracking even standard/file-synced content (DocType, Report) rather than relying on module-sync alone.<br><br>**Reuse, precisely scoped**: 100% of the backend is untouched — `proc_portal.api.rfqs.get_comparison_sheet()` / `get_purchase_orders_for_rfq()` / `get_comparison_sheet_actions()` and the four selection/workflow/PO-generation action endpoints are called as-is via `frappe.call()`, no new backend method anywhere. The route gives a *sheet* name; since `get_comparison_sheet()` takes an *RFQ* name, the page resolves one to the other client-side via a single `frappe.db.get_value()` read rather than adding a new backend method for it. The portal's two Alpine components (`poGenActions()`, `comparisonActions()`) were ported near-verbatim — same state shape, same endpoints, same args — with two deliberate transport-layer adaptations, not business-logic changes: `frappe.call()` in place of raw `fetch()` + manual CSRF-header/`_server_messages` parsing (desk's own call wrapper already does both), and a scoped `render()` re-fetch-and-redraw in place of `window.location.reload()` after a successful action (a full reload would reload the entire desk SPA, not just this page). The Jinja-rendered list/table markup itself (400 lines in `rfq-comparison.html`) has no desk equivalent to inherit, so it was ported to JS template-literal builder functions (`build_proposal_table`, `build_item_summary_table`, `build_item_detail_tables`, `build_po_section`) preserving the exact same structure, conditionals, and per-row logic — verified faithful via the runtime test below, not just eyeballed.<br><br>**CRITICAL, found-not-assumed cascade collisions** (checked by grepping `portal.css` for bare/unscoped selectors, not assumed absent): `* { box-sizing: border-box; }` is a real global rule but confirmed **harmless** — grepped the compiled `desk.bundle.css` and found Bootstrap's own reboot already sets the identical rule on `*, *::before, *::after`, so this is a redundant no-op. `body { background/color/font-family/font-size/line-height/margin }`, however, is a **real, serious** collision: `frappe.require()`'s `AssetManager` (confirmed by reading `frappe/public/js/frappe/assets.js`) appends a `<link>` and never removes it for the life of the desk session, and desk's own compiled bundle sets real, different values for exactly these properties (`font-size: 16px`, `font-family: var(--font-stack)`) — without a fix, visiting this page once would permanently re-theme every other desk screen's body typography/background for the rest of the browser session, not just this page. Fixed with a second stylesheet (`proc_app/public/css/rfq_comparison_page.css`) loaded immediately after `portal.css` in the same `frappe.require()` array — `AssetManager.execute()`'s `items.map()` appends `<link>` tags synchronously in array order (confirmed by reading the source, not assumed), so cascade-tie ordering is guaranteed regardless of network fetch timing — that reverts `body`'s properties (`revert` keyword, robust to desk's own light/dark theme switching, unlike hardcoding desk's literal values) and re-applies the portal's typography only inside `.rfq-comparison-page`. A third, smaller collision found the same way: Bootstrap's bare `label { margin-bottom: 0.5rem; }` wasn't overridden by the item-selection radio's own inline style (which didn't set `margin`), so it would have picked up an unwanted 8px gap the portal version doesn't have — fixed inline (`margin:0`) rather than documented-and-left, since it was a one-line, unambiguous fix. No class-name collisions at all: every `.rich-*`/`.portal-*`/`.detail-*`/`.status-*`/`.item-picker-*` selector in `portal.css` was grepped against the compiled desk bundle and found zero matches.<br><br>**Alpine-in-desk mechanics, verified against the real CDN bundle** (`alpinejs@3.x.x` resolves to `3.17.1`, fetched directly rather than assumed): the initial premise that `Alpine.start()` needs manual re-triggering because `DOMContentLoaded` has already fired was **wrong** — grepped the actual bundle and found no `deferLoadingAlpine` mechanism at all in this version; Alpine auto-starts unconditionally when its script executes and sets up a `MutationObserver` that auto-detects and initialises any later-inserted `x-data` subtree on its own. `Alpine.initTree()`/`Alpine.destroyTree()` (both confirmed present in the real bundle) are used anyway for determinism rather than relying purely on the async observer: `render()` calls `Alpine.destroyTree()` on the outgoing markup before replacing it and `Alpine.initTree()` on the incoming markup right after — safe against both double-init (`frappe.require()`'s `AssetManager._executed` tracking makes re-loading Alpine on `refresh` a no-op) and stale reactive state across re-visits.<br><br>**A real bug found and fixed by the runtime test, not by inspection**: every `x-data`/`@click`/`@change` attribute that embedded a `JSON.stringify()`-produced string (e.g. `x-data="proc_app_poGenActions(' + JSON.stringify(rfq_name) + ')"`) was double-quote-delimited HTML wrapping a double-quoted JS string literal — the first `"` inside the JSON output silently truncated the HTML attribute. Static inspection and `node --check` (which only validates the *building* JS, not the *HTML string it produces*) both passed cleanly; the bug only surfaced when the actual produced markup was fed to the real Alpine bundle in a jsdom runtime test, which reported `Alpine Expression Error: Unexpected token '}' — Expression: "proc_app_poGenActions("` (silently truncated, no arguments). Fixed with a `js_attr()` helper (`esc(JSON.stringify(v))`) used at all 5 call sites; the browser correctly HTML-decodes `&quot;` back to `"` before Alpine evaluates the expression as JS.<br><br>**Runtime verification (jsdom + the real, fetched `alpinejs@3.17.1` bundle, not a mock)**: extracted the actual `build_*` functions unmodified from the shipped file, fed them a data shape matching the live cross-check from v1.66's report task (including one genuinely ineligible supplier, to exercise the "Incomplete — missing N of M items" branch this project has not yet been able to test live against real site data) — `build_page_html()` ran with no exceptions and produced the expected root class, both `x-data` roots, and the correct incomplete-status text; `Alpine.initTree()` ran with zero console errors; a real synthetic click on the mode-toggle correctly flipped both `x-show` bindings (`item` → hidden, `proposal` → visible) once given a full tick for Alpine's microtask-batched reactivity scheduler to flush (an initial too-short wait produced a false negative in the test itself, not a real page bug — Alpine's actual browser-visible update is imperceptibly fast to a human, this was purely a test-harness timing artifact); `Alpine.destroyTree()` ran cleanly with no exceptions, confirming the `refresh()` re-render path is safe.<br><br>**Two extensions applied beyond the task's literal ask, flagged rather than silently added**: (1) the task described updating "the Comparison Sheet's existing Client Script" and "the RFQ form's own View button" as if two separate buttons needed the route change — investigation found there is genuinely only **one** "View Comparison Sheet" button in the whole system (on the RFQ form's own script; the Comparison Sheet's own script has an unrelated "By Proposal Comparison" button routing to the v1.66 report, left untouched). Only that one real button was changed, from `frappe.set_route('Form', 'RFQ Comparison Sheet', ...)` to `frappe.set_route('rfq-comparison', ...)`. (2) The same RFQ script's Generate/Regenerate Comparison Sheet buttons' post-action navigation was also repointed to the new page (previously routed to the standard form) — not explicitly requested, but leaving it pointed at the old form would have been visibly inconsistent immediately after View was changed to go elsewhere. |

| 1.45 | 2026-08-29 | KCSC | **Incident report — real data loss caused by a test cleanup script, found and fully recovered same session.** While verifying v1.44's attachment-copy fix, the test's own cleanup step force-deleted `MAT-MR-2026-00025` — a real, unrelated, in-progress Purchase-type Material Request (genuinely spawned minutes earlier via a real Forward-to-Purchase transition on the real, still-live `MAT-MR-2026-00023`), not test debris. **Root cause, not a fluke**: the cleanup filter scoped by `requesting_department = "Accounts - K"` AND `creation within the last N minutes` — a combination that can match any real, unrelated document sharing the same department and falling inside the same short window as the test run, which is exactly what happened here. **Recovery**: Frappe's own `Deleted Document` audit log had preserved a complete, faithful JSON snapshot of the original record. The native `frappe.core.doctype.deleted_document.deleted_document.restore()` utility was tried first but correctly rejected the data — this document's `workflow_state` had been set via the spawn hook's own `db_set()` bypass (a system-driven, non-transition state change, the same reason `on_material_request_update()` itself has to use `db_set()` rather than a normal workflow transition), and `restore()`'s plain `doc.insert()` re-validates the workflow transition graph, which has no valid path landing directly on that state for a brand-new document. Recovered manually instead, mirroring the exact same established pattern already used by the spawn hook: inserted fresh at the workflow's real initial state (`Draft`, with `workflow_state` unset), renamed to the original `MAT-MR-2026-00025` (confirmed genuinely free first, via a direct SQL count), then `db_set()` the true `workflow_state`, `owner`, `creation`, and `docstatus` back from the backup. **Verified field-by-field against the backup, not just "it exists again"**: name, owner, creation timestamp, docstatus, workflow_state, `source_material_request`, both departments, and all 3 real item rows (`SKU010`×4, `SKU006`×5, `SKU001`×10 — quantities, rates, and warehouses each independently compared) all matched exactly. Separately confirmed the real source document, `MAT-MR-2026-00023`, was never touched throughout (`docstatus=1`, `workflow_state=Forwarded to Purchase`, unchanged before and after). **Standing rule for this project going forward**: a cleanup filter in any test script that operates against this bench (a shared, live environment with genuine in-progress user data, not an isolated test database) must scope by something that cannot match real, unrelated documents — an exact document name or an explicit list of IDs captured at creation time — never a broad field-plus-timeframe combination, no matter how narrow the timeframe looks at a glance. A department, a status, or a "created recently" window are all real values genuine documents can share purely by coincidence. |

Confirmed via the actual delivery endpoint (`frappe.desk.form.load.getdoctype`), not just source reading: the broken version's doc appeared in the returned meta bundle (confirming `get_meta_bundle()` does iterate and build metas for a parent's child-table doctypes) but its `__custom_js` was empty; the corrected version's content showed up as `True`. Cross-checked against this project's own history: every previously-working Client Script (`RFQ Comparison Sheet Item Supplier Ellipsis`, `Request for Quotation Desk Buttons`) was already attached to a parent (`istable=0`) doctype — this was the first, and only, attempt to attach one directly to a child table, and it was the one that broke. **Correct, standard Frappe idiom** (now proven, not assumed): attach the `Client Script` to the *parent* doctype (`dt: "Supplier Quotation"`), with `frappe.ui.form.on('Supplier Quotation Item', {...})` still registered *inside* that script for the child-row field trigger — the handler-registration target and the script's own delivery-scope (`dt`) are two separate things, and only the latter is constrained by `istable`. Cleaned up the broken record (deleted from DB and confirmed absent from the re-exported fixture: `0` occurrences) before creating the corrected one (`Supplier Quotation Lead Time Auto-Calc`), independently reconfirmed present in the fixture (`1` occurrence) and delivered via the real endpoint. | 1.31 | 2026-08-27 | KCSC | **CONFIRMED WORKING — final resolution** of the `RFQ Comparison Sheet Item` desk-grid decimal-display issue, closing out an investigation spanning many sessions (v1.23 → v1.31), and confirmed not by server-side inference this time but by Yasser's own hands-on browser test with a real screenshot showing consistent decimals across every row (`88.00`, `100.00`, `68.57`, `88.89`, `0.00`, `53.33`). The full arc, recorded here as the reusable lesson it is: **(1) Root cause (v1.26)** — `price_score`/`lead_time_score`/`weighted_mark` had no explicit `precision`, so Frappe's `Float` formatter trimmed trailing zeros per-value, producing genuinely different decimal-place counts in the same column. **(2) First fix attempt failed (v1.26→v1.27)** — setting `precision=2` alone didn't work, because `Float`'s formatter (`frappe/public/js/frappe/form/formatters.js`) has a hardcoded `// show 1.000000 as 1` block that overrides any explicit `precision` back to `0` whenever a value's own decimal part is missing or zero. **(3) Currency dead-end investigated and correctly abandoned (v1.27)** — switching fieldtype to `Currency` would have inherited fixed-precision behavior, but the only symbol-suppression mechanism in Frappe (`hide_currency_symbol`) is a global, site-wide default with no per-field equivalent (confirmed via source and a live `ValidationError` when queried on `System Settings`) — would have hidden currency symbols on every real monetary field sitewide, an unacceptable tradeoff, reported honestly before proceeding rather than worked around silently. **(4) Client Script formatter built (v1.27)** — fields stayed `Float`, a Client Script forcing `parseFloat(value).toFixed(2)` via `grid.update_docfield_property(fieldname, 'formatter', fn)` was the correct mechanism (confirmed via exhaustive source tracing in a dedicated follow-up investigation: `grid.refresh()` → `render_result_rows()` → `grid_row.refresh()` → `render_row(true)` → `setup_columns()` → `refresh_field()` → `column.static_area.html(txt)` — every step in this chain does correctly re-consult `df.formatter` on a plain refresh, and a second investigation confirmed the script itself reaches the browser correctly via the real `frappe.desk.form.load.getdoctype` delivery mechanism, no caching bug). **(5) The actual remaining gap — real-world timing, not mechanism** — despite the formatter being correctly attached and proven correct when called manually (Yasser's own test: produced `'88.00'` on demand), the grid cell kept showing `'88'` after ordinary page load. Per source, `update_docfield_property()`'s own `debounced_refresh()` should have caught this automatically — but empirically, on real initial page load, it wasn't reliably firing at the right moment relative to the grid's own initial render. **Final fix**: the Client Script now explicitly calls `frm.fields_dict['items'].grid.refresh()` itself, wrapped in a 300ms `setTimeout` as a pragmatic guard against this exact timing gap, rather than relying solely on the implicit refresh triggered inside `update_docfield_property()`. Verified via the same discipline used throughout this fix's history: computed the line-based insertion result *before* saving (not blindly trusting the string-splice logic, per the standing lesson from the earlier `rfind()` placement bug) to confirm the `setTimeout` block landed correctly inside `refresh: function(frm) { ... }`, not outside it; validated with `node --check` against both the pre-save draft and the actual saved document content (exit 0). **Confirmed working by the person who actually needed it working**, not just server-side checks — the single most reliable verification this whole saga could have had. |

Root cause: the prior task's own closing `bench migrate` (run to confirm Stage B's `after_migrate()` change didn't break a clean cycle) re-imported `workflow.json` via `sync_fixtures()` — and since the permanent pre-commit hook (v1.17) always resets the *committed fixture* to `is_active=0` before every commit, that correctly-zeroed fixture gets *re-applied to the live DB* on the very next migrate, deactivating the real workflows. **This is a previously undocumented consequence of the v1.17 mitigation's actual scope**: the hook keeps the git-committed fixture clean (which is correct and working as intended), but does not — and structurally cannot — keep the live DB's workflows active across a migrate, since migrate legitimately imports whatever the fixture says. Fixed by reactivating all 3 via `.save()` (not raw `db.set_value()`, per standing cache-invalidation policy), independently reconfirmed via fresh SQL. | 1.27 | 2026-08-27 | KCSC | Final resolution of the desk-grid decimal-display inconsistency, closing out an investigation spanning several sessions (v1.23 ruled out column width/currency; v1.26 found and attempted to fix via `precision=2`). v1.26's fix did not actually work: Yasser's screenshot still showed `88` alongside `68.57`/`53.33` in the same column. Root-caused via `frappe/public/js/frappe/form/formatters.js` source: `Float`'s formatter contains a deliberate `// show 1.000000 as 1` block that overrides the field's own `precision` to `0` whenever a value's decimal part is missing or zero (unless the caller passes `options.always_show_decimals`, which grid cell rendering does not) — `Currency`'s formatter has no equivalent override. Considered switching these 3 fields to `Currency` (Yasser's initial decision) specifically to inherit that fixed-precision behavior, but first investigated whether the currency symbol could be suppressed per-field, since a `JOD` label next to a lead-time score would be confusing — confirmed via source (`number_format.js`, `data.py`) and via a live runtime check that `hide_currency_symbol` is a **global, site-wide default only** (`frappe.boot.sysdefaults.hide_currency_symbol` / `frappe.defaults.get_global_default(...)`), with no per-docfield equivalent at all (`DocField`'s own schema has no such field; `System Settings` doesn't either, confirmed by a real `ValidationError` when queried). Flipping it would have hidden currency symbols on every monetary field sitewide — an unacceptable tradeoff, reported honestly before proceeding rather than silently working around it. **Final fix**: fields stay `Float` with `precision=2` (untouched, still correct at the data-model level); extended the existing Supplier-ellipsis `Client Script` with a second formatter block, applying `parseFloat(value).toFixed(2)` to `price_score`/`lead_time_score`/`weighted_mark` via the same `update_docfield_property(fieldname, 'formatter', ...)` mechanism already proven in v1.24 — a pure client-side display fix, no data-model change. **Caught a real placement bug before it shipped**: the originally-proposed string-splice approach (`current_script.rfind("});")`) targeted the *last* `});` in the script, which closes the outer `frappe.ui.form.on(...)` call, not the inner one closing `refresh`'s existing formatter — inserting there would have placed the new code as a bare statement outside the `refresh` function, invalid JavaScript. Confirmed this precisely by computing the spliced string without saving it first: both brace-count and paren-count checks passed anyway (they're insensitive to structural placement), which would have wrongly signaled "OK" — exactly the false-positive risk flagged in this task's own instructions. Rewrote the script cleanly instead, with both formatters correctly nested inside the same `refresh` function; verified with `node --check` against the actual saved document content (not just a local draft) before and after, confirming genuine syntactic validity beyond brace/paren counting. Verified nothing server-side was affected: raw stored values and fieldtype (`Float`) on `RFQCS-2026-00001` unchanged before and after. Fixture-exported (already covered by the existing `Client Script` filter from v1.24) and a subsequent `migrate` confirmed clean (exit 0, no orphans). **Same visual-verification limitation as v1.24**: this can be confirmed server-side as syntactically valid and correctly persisted, but the actual rendered decimal alignment in the desk grid still needs Yasser's visual confirmation in-browser. |
| 1.26 | 2026-08-27 | KCSC | Found and fixed the actual root cause of the recurring desk-grid alignment complaint on `RFQ Comparison Sheet Item` — not the column-width issue already fixed in v1.23 (real, but not the whole story): `price_score`, `lead_time_score`, `weighted_mark` were created as plain `Float` fields with no explicit `precision`, so Frappe displays each value with its own natural (trailing-zero-trimmed) decimal length rather than a uniform one. Confirmed directly against `RFQCS-2026-00001`'s real stored data before touching anything: in the same `weighted_mark` column, MA Inc. displayed as `88.0` (1 decimal) while Zuckerman Security Ltd. and Summit Traders Ltd. displayed as `68.57`/`53.33` (2 decimals) — a genuine, row-to-row text-length difference in a right-aligned numeric column, independently of column width. Fixed by setting `precision=2` on all 3 fields directly on the `DocType`, matching how native ERPNext Currency/Float columns (which always set explicit precision) stay visually uniform. Fixture-exported (already covered by the existing `RFQ Comparison Sheet Item` `DocType` filter, no `hooks.py` change needed) and a subsequent `migrate` confirmed clean (exit 0, no orphans). Full mechanism explained in Known Issues, framed generally — this applies to any custom Float/Currency field in this project, not just these 3. |

Root cause: the new Module Def "Proc App" was created directly in the database but `apps/proc_app/proc_app/modules.txt` was never updated to list it, so migrate's doctype-discovery scan never recognized the moved files' location as valid, and its orphan-cleanup pass deleted the now-unreachable `tabDocType` records (the underlying DB tables themselves were never dropped — confirmed via `SHOW TABLES`, and since all 3 doctypes had 0 rows to begin with, no actual data was lost). Fixed by adding `Proc App` to `modules.txt`. (2) A second migrate still didn't re-register the doctypes — `proc_app` didn't even appear in the "Updating DocTypes for X" sync list. Root cause, confirmed via `frappe.scrub()`/`get_module_path()` rather than assumed: the module "Proc App" scrubs to `proc_app`, and Frappe expects that module's doctypes at `apps/proc_app/proc_app/proc_app/doctype/...` — a third `proc_app` nesting level, matching the same pattern `supplier_portal/supplier_portal/supplier_portal/` already used — but the files had been placed one level shallower, at `apps/proc_app/proc_app/doctype/...`. Fixed by moving the 3 folders one level deeper to match `get_module_path()`'s actual expected location. A third migrate then correctly synced `proc_app` for the first time, registered all 3 `tabDocType` records under module "Proc App", with zero orphans. Verified via functional smoke tests: created and deleted a real `Supplier ASN` (against a real submitted PO) and a real `Supplier Invoice Dispute` (against a real submitted Purchase Invoice, with a corrected `dispute_type` select value after an initial wrong-test-data failure) — both succeeded cleanly post-move. Confirmed supplier_portal's existing API code (`asn.py`, `invoices.py`) references these doctypes by name only (`"doctype": "Supplier ASN"` style), never by direct Python import path — zero code changes needed there, since Frappe resolves doctypes by name/module in the database regardless of which app's folder currently contains them. Custom field migration (7 fields: 3 on Purchase Order, 4 on Supplier) and the API/hooks business-logic split (`acknowledge_po`, `submit_asn`, `submit_invoice`, `submit_dispute`, `request_amendment`, plus `hooks_handlers.py`) are deliberately deferred as a separate follow-up task, not bundled into this stage. |

## 7. Deployment Procedure

**Verified 2026-09-08 against a genuinely fresh site** (`deploytest.local`, created for exactly this purpose and deliberately kept around for verifying future fixture changes) — not reasoned about, actually installed end to end and inventoried. The correct, minimal command sequence for installing this project on any fresh site:

```bash
bench new-site <sitename> --admin-password <pw> --mariadb-root-password '<root-pw>'
bench --site <sitename> install-app erpnext
bench --site <sitename> install-app proc_app
bench --site <sitename> install-app proc_portal
bench --site <sitename> install-app supplier_portal
bench --site <sitename> migrate
```

**The final `bench migrate` is not optional, and not just good practice — it's mandatory.** `after_migrate` (`proc_app.setup.after_migrate`, registered in `hooks.py`) is what actually runs every `setup_*_permissions()` function in `setup.py` — the entire Custom DocPerm permission model for Procurement Officer/Department Manager/Officer/User (51 grants) is configured there, not via any fixture. **Confirmed live, not assumed: `after_migrate` does not fire during `bench install-app` at all** — only `bench migrate` triggers it. Skipping the final migrate leaves every one of this project's roles with zero real permissions on any doctype, even though `install-app` itself reports success.

**Workflows arrive `is_active: 0` on purpose** (a standing, deliberate policy enforced by this repo's own pre-commit hook, not drift — see the 2026-08-27 Known Issues entry) — a human must explicitly activate the workflow(s) relevant to the deployment after this sequence completes, the same as on every site this project has ever set up.

**History**: this exact command sequence used to fail outright on `proc_app`'s install — see the 2026-09-08 Known Issues entries below for the full root-cause investigation (a stray `Page` fixture entry) and the fix (Changelog v1.95). If this sequence ever fails again, `deploytest.local` (or a fresh site made the same way) is the way to find out why — reasoning about what fixtures *should* contain has twice now missed real, structural problems that only a genuine install surfaced.

*This document is the single source of truth for the KCSC Proc project. All team members and AI coding sessions must keep it current.*
